The Ogg packet-counter reachability gate passed on 2026-08-03. Memory readers now reject a logical-stream packet count beyond their global count. Positional readers may retain different counter origins after seeking, but they now allow a final packet to reach the saturated counter state, reject any further packet before committing owner state, and require pending seek skips to fit the remaining logical counter range. Direct and installed-package regressions cover impossible counters, saturation, overflow, state preservation, and the public package boundary. The focused Ogg and Vorbis suite passes 90/90 tests and ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. The independent interoperability gate passes 11/11 steps and 95/95 tests with the expected unavailable AudioToolbox decoder skip. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,328/7,334 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, decoder exchange, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required for these retained-counter invariants.

The FLAC positional-reader audio-extent gate passed on 2026-08-03. Retained validation now derives the minimum and maximum possible encoded-frame counts from total PCM frames and STREAMINFO block bounds, then relates the audio byte extent to declared nonzero frame-size extrema. Impossible restored or corrupted byte extents fail before positional I/O. Direct and installed-package regressions cover the retained owner, unchanged decode output, and the public package boundary. The focused FLAC suite passes 38/38 tests and ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,328/7,334 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, decoder exchange, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required for this retained-state invariant.

The bounded FLAC parser audio-extent gate passed on 2026-08-03. In-memory parsing now applies the same block-count and declared frame-size proof before decoding the first frame, so an impossible STREAMINFO extent cannot partially change ordinary decoder output. A lower-bound failure retains the established `TruncatedFlac` result, while an impossible upper bound returns `InvalidFlacFrameSize`. Direct and installed-package regressions corrupt only the frame-size extrema and verify unchanged output through the public decoder. The focused FLAC suite passes 38/38 tests and ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,328/7,334 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, decoder exchange, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required for this pre-decode invariant.

The FLAC seek-point progress gate passed on 2026-08-03. Memory and positional validation now derive the possible number of complete frames preceding each seek sample from STREAMINFO block bounds, then constrain its audio-relative byte offset with any declared frame-size extrema. A seek point at sample one cannot claim the first frame byte when the minimum block holds sixteen samples. Direct and installed-package regressions cover malformed retained progress, unchanged range output, and the public package boundary. The focused FLAC suite passes 38/38 tests and ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,328/7,334 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, decoder exchange, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required for this seek invariant.

The FLAC seek-point block-length gate passed on 2026-08-03. Shared memory and positional validation now permit a seek point shorter than STREAMINFO's minimum block size only when that point reaches the exact terminal sample. Direct and installed-package regressions cover a forged short first frame, unchanged range output, and the public package boundary. The focused FLAC suite passes 38/38 tests and ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,328/7,334 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, decoder exchange, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required for this seek invariant.

The FLAC seek-offset ordering gate passed on 2026-08-03. Every active seek point must now advance both its sample number and audio-relative byte offset, so corrupted retained metadata cannot bind a later sample to the same or an earlier encoded frame. Direct regressions cover metadata validation, the positional reader, unchanged range output, and the installed public iterator boundary. The focused FLAC suite passes 38/38 tests and ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,328/7,334 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, decoder exchange, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required for this seek invariant.

The FLAC seek-target header gate passed on 2026-08-03. Memory and positional initialization now parse each targeted frame header, validate its CRC, and require its coded frame or sample number and block length to match the seek entry. The same header parser serves normal decoding, which keeps the two contracts synchronized. Direct and installed-package regressions corrupt a structurally plausible seek sample while preserving output sentinels. The focused FLAC suite passes 38/38 tests and ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,328/7,334 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, decoder exchange, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required for this seek invariant.

The focused LV2 closing pass on 2026-08-02 supersedes earlier same-day rows that describe the installed runtime as unreadable or all semantic compile gates as pending. Core and metadata tests pass 555/555 through the dynamic-topology gate, the component-state and toolkit-neutral UI loadable host fixtures pass, the independent C ABI harness passes, all 12 supported cross-target libraries compile, and installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps. The complete ReleaseSafe pass above also includes the aggregate LV2 gate.

The LV2 UI host-value request pass on 2026-08-03 adds the standard optional asynchronous request feature behind a format-neutral URI contract. Direct GUI and adapter tests cover URI validation, exact key and optional type mapping, standard status propagation, unrecognized status containment, missing callbacks, null callbacks, and duplicate-feature disabling. The dynamically loaded UI fixture exercises the shared-library callback, while separately compiled C checks the status width, structure layout, alignment, and field offsets. The focused LV2 gate passes 56/56 steps and 559/559 tests across native and supported cross-target builds. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps and repository policy checks pass. The complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,316/7,322 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No manual smoke or visual confirmation is required for the implementation itself. External-host request behavior remains open.

The LV2 UI resize-metadata pass on 2026-08-03 adds explicit resizable, programmatic-only, and fixed behavior to the public metadata contract. Resizable output retains host and UI resize claims. Programmatic-only output additionally prevents user resizing. Fixed output advertises the standard fixed-size feature and omits both resize claims. Direct generation tests check all three exact combinations, and installed-package consumers exercise the fixed public path. The focused LV2 gate passes 56/56 steps and 559/559 tests across native and supported cross-target builds. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps, repository policy checks pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,316/7,322 tests with six environment-dependent skips. External-host discovery remains open and no visual result is inferred.

The LV2 UI peak-subscription complete gate passed on 2026-08-03. Toolkit-neutral editors can subscribe by validated stable port symbol and receive format-neutral source IDs with standard period and peak data. The LV2 bridge maps symbols to host port indexes, retains at most 16 accepted subscriptions without allocation, treats exact repeats idempotently, rejects port and source-ID collisions, validates copied payloads, and automatically unsubscribes during cleanup and partial-construction rollback. Missing or malformed optional feature records leave the UI usable without peak support. Generated metadata advertises port map, port subscription, and peak protocol support. Direct UI tests pass 281/281, metadata tests pass 263/263, the loadable host and independent C ABI harness pass, and installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps. The focused LV2 matrix passes 56/56 steps and 559/559 tests across native and supported cross-target builds. Repository policies pass. The complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,318/7,324 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. External-host peak delivery remains open. No visual confirmation is inferred.

The native LV2 meter-integration automated gate passed on 2026-08-03. The VSTGUI LV2 backend accepts up to 16 declarative peak sources and eight meter descriptions, subscribes during editor creation, stores validated measurements per editor, and exposes them through the existing native meter load callback. Compile-time checks reject duplicate symbols, duplicate source IDs, excessive counts, and unknown meter sources. Mono Gain now declares input and output peak meters. The backend-focused suite passes 68/68, the LV2 matrix passes 56/56 steps with 559/559 tests including the native UI bundle and host fixture, installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps, and repository policies pass. The complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,318/7,324 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. A real LV2 host must confirm live meter delivery, and manual visual confirmation must check meter labels, placement, motion, peak hold, reset, scaling, and editor reopen behavior.

The LV2 static UI-notification complete gate passed on 2026-08-03. Public UI metadata emits declaration-driven `ui:portNotification` entries with float delivery for control ports or peak delivery for audio ports by stable symbol. Validation rejects malformed, unknown, protocol-incompatible, and duplicate symbols before emitting output. Peak sources explicitly select static or dynamic delivery. Static sources require port mapping and peak-protocol negotiation but neither invoke nor clean up through the dynamic port-subscription callback. The shipped Mono Gain UI declares gain updates and static input and output peak measurements. The loadable toolkit-neutral UI independently registers and receives a static peak with no port-subscription feature, then proves cleanup does not call the missing dynamic callback. Direct GUI tests pass 20/20, the native VSTGUI LV2 backend passes 68/68, the focused LV2 matrix passes 56/56 steps and 559/559 tests, installed-package consumers pass 12/12 steps and 82/82 tests, and repository policies pass. The complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,318/7,324 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. The bundle generator and structural script checks pass. The external schema and lint command could not rerun because `lv2_validate` is unavailable, so independent validation of the changed Turtle remains open. Real-host discovery and delivery remain open. Manual meter appearance and motion remain in the LV2 interoperability row.

The LV2 typed UI Atom-notification gate passed on 2026-08-03. Public GUI contracts register a stable event-output symbol, exact Atom type URI, and format-neutral source ID, then deliver a borrowed bounded message body to the backend. The LV2 bridge requires port mapping, URI mapping, and the standard event-transfer protocol, retains at most 16 bindings, and rejects ambiguous source or port-and-type assignments. Exact complete Atom packets up to 64 KiB are accepted. Type zero, truncation, trailing bytes, oversized bodies, unknown types, wrong ports, and wrong formats are ignored. Metadata emits typed event notifications and permits distinct types on one output. The loadable shared-library fixture receives an exact body, installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps, the C ABI harness passes, all 12 LV2 cross-target libraries compile, and the focused LV2 matrix passes 56/56 steps and 560/560 tests. Repository policies pass. The complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,321/7,327 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. External schema and warning-fatal lint validation of event-transfer metadata plus real-host typed delivery remain open. No manual visual confirmation is required for this transport capability.

The LV2 typed editor-to-plugin Atom gate passed on 2026-08-03. `PluginAtomMessage` identifies one event input and Atom type through validated stable strings and borrows at most 64 KiB of body storage for one UI-thread call. `UiMetadata.event_input_messages` explicitly advertises the event-transfer protocol and is rejected when the generated core has no event input. The bridge maps the symbol and type, creates one complete 64-bit-aligned Atom, rejects a reentrant write, calls the host write function, and erases the instance-owned packet immediately afterward. The loadable fixture now uses the actual generated event-input, event-output, and control indexes, checks the outbound packet across the shared-library boundary, and attempts a nested exported write from inside the host callback. Direct tests cover invalid strings, oversize bodies, missing support, unknown symbols and types, outbound-only metadata, exact framing, reentrancy, and scratch erasure. The focused LV2 matrix passes 56/56 steps and 560/560 tests, all 12 cross-target libraries compile, and installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps. Repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,321/7,327 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. Independent metadata validation and real-host delivery remain open. No manual visual confirmation is required.

The LV2 retained optional-state hardening gate passed on 2026-08-03. Worker scheduling, output resizing, host logging, state-change notification, URI reverse mapping, and Programs descriptor storage now use explicit empty optionals until their binding path publishes a value. The native LV2 editor backend likewise keeps its editor pointer empty until native creation succeeds. Adapter paths that need the native editor reject or ignore work until that publication. `OwnedStatePath.deinit` clears its host pointer and validated length after the first release, making later `bytes` and `deinit` calls deterministic and preventing a duplicate host free. Direct LV2 tests pass 294/294, the focused LV2 matrix passes 56/56 steps and 560/560 tests, all 12 cross-target libraries compile, and installed-package consumers pass 82/82 tests across 12/12 steps. Repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,321/7,327 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. No manual, host, device, audible, or visual confirmation is required for these retained-state invariants.

The HRTF runtime-loader ownership gate passed on 2026-08-03. The optional netCDF library and its resolved function table now have explicit closed states. `isOpen` requires both owners to be live, teardown clears callable symbols before releasing the library and is safe to repeat, and every load after teardown reports `SofaLoaderClosed` before allocation or destination mutation. The private Windows library owner follows the same repeatable close contract. Direct regressions cover a closed loader, allocation-order evidence, unchanged output, repeated teardown, and teardown after an optional public dataset load. The focused HRTF gate passes 6/6 steps and 142/144 tests with only the two optional public datasets skipped, including ReleaseSafe compilation for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,322/7,328 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. The adjacent public owned-storage audit found deterministic release states in the matrix decompositions, byte accumulator, resource exchange, recovery pipeline, and audio buffer. No manual, host, device, audible, or visual confirmation is required for the loader lifecycle invariant. Loading an installed netCDF DLL on Windows and dataset audition remain separately tracked external checks.

The processor-runtime terminal-state gate passed on 2026-08-03. `ProcessorRuntime` now distinguishes released storage from a newly initialized instance. Teardown completes any active lifecycle work, clears retained preparation configuration, publishes `deinitialized`, and calls the product destructor once. Repeated teardown is a no-op. Fallible lifecycle, state, automation, and processing operations report `ProcessorDeinitialized`; latency and tail queries return zero without reading released product state. Terminal state takes precedence over unsupported sample precision. The offline renderer, standalone runtime, and standalone application delegate teardown to this owner and inherit its repeatable terminal behavior. Their surrounding configuration and running markers already collapse before or during release, and the standalone window has its own guarded destroyed state. A direct regression uses a deliberately non-repeatable product destructor backed by the testing allocator. It exercises every public operation after teardown, repeats teardown, and reconstructs the runtime in the same storage. Installed-package coverage repeats the public lifecycle contract. Plugin-core cross-compilation passes 2/2 steps for Windows x86-64 GNU, installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps, repository policies pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 398/398 steps and 7,324/7,330 tests with six expected environment-dependent skips. No manual, host, device, audible, or visual confirmation is required.

Live interoperability requiring FFmpeg or an independent encoder remains open where a row says so.

An out-of-range position reports `InvalidAudioBuffer` instead of the misleading `SourceUnavailable`; malformed retained page dimensions, a missing later page, and zero-length channel mismatches fail without changing any output. Refresh replaces each resident logical page in its existing slot instead of leaving an older duplicate directory entry that could shadow the new generation. Checked mapping rejects timestamp rollback, timestamps before the anchor, unresolvable same-sample updates, sample-position overflow, malformed observations, and malformed retained configuration without advancing state. A future `reanchor` requires both clocks to advance. Tests cover exact and drifted calibration, exact anchor and half-second mapping, future reanchoring, invalid observations and rates, clock rollback, same-sample timestamps, failed-reanchor rollback, recovery after failures, sample-position overflow, and hostile retained fields. The complete public DSP module passed 995/995 before the calibration addition, then the focused public HRTF surface passed 15/15. Installed-package consumers passed 63/63 across 12/12 steps, ReleaseSafe DSP compilation passed for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU, and the expanded local DSP thread-sanitizer gate passed 55/55 tests. Hosted run `30684646626` passed on macOS, Ubuntu, and Windows. Its Ubuntu realtime DSP concurrency job passed 55/55 tests under ThreadSanitizer, closing calibrated-clock hosted validation. The later robust-calibration gate closes bounded observation filtering, and the streaming-renderer gate closes fixed-slot filter publication. Device timestamp acquisition and synchronized observation capture remain product integration work |
| Verification gate for bounded HRTF tracker transport | Focused, public DSP, installed-package, cross-target, local thread-sanitizer, and hosted thread-sanitizer verification passed 2026-08-01 | `HrtfMotionPointQueue(capacity)` provides one-producer, one-consumer transport for absolute-sample source and head poses. Submission validates pose geometry and strict time order before publication. Tracked submission composes clock mapping and queue publication transactionally, so full queues and invalid poses leave the producer clock retryable. Full queues preserve retained points and report a saturating drop count. Receive and pending-count operations reject malformed cursor distances without reading storage. Reset is explicitly quiescent-only. Focused tests cover order, full-queue retry, combined clock retry, invalid-pose and timestamp rollback, index wrap, corrupt cursors, saturated drops, and 4,096 updates transferred concurrently. The public DSP module passed 995/995 tests, installed-package consumers passed 63/63 across 12/12 steps, ReleaseSafe DSP compilation passed for Linux AArch64, Linux x86-64, and Windows x86-64 GNU, and hosted Ubuntu run `30683990773` passed the expanded DSP thread-sanitizer gate with 54/54 tests. The later clock-calibration and streaming-renderer gates close bounded synchronization, filter preparation, and audio-thread publication. Device acquisition and preparation-executor selection remain product integration work |
| Verification gate for Vorbis comment metadata exchange | Focused, orchestration, and hosted external-encoder gates passed 2026-07-31 | The bounded decoder probe can require one case-insensitive comment name with an exact UTF-8 value. The project fixture proves successful typed lookup and rejection of an incorrect value. The FFmpeg fixture writes a title through the independent encoder and exposes that exact value through the project comment iterator. The fake-tool runner requires the project and external metadata calls, their success reports, and every downstream probe. Hosted Ubuntu run `30674942630` passed the independent FFmpeg comment-metadata exchange |
| Verification gate for checksum-correct Vorbis packet damage | Strict hosted rejection and explicit hosted recovery passed 2026-08-01 | The damage generator locates the first audio packet after the three Vorbis headers, sets its packet-type bit, and recomputes the complete Ogg page CRC. The ordinary decoder then rejects the stream specifically as `InvalidVorbisAudioPacketType`, proving packet validation rather than transport checksum rejection. Hosted Ubuntu run `30689183600` passed both independently encoded invalid-audio-packet rejection and the explicit known-block concealment path. The recovery probe conceals exactly one invalid packet and decodes later packets to finite nonsilent PCM. Its fake-tool matrix proves both rejection and recovery calls are mandatory. Separate policies infer geometry from preceding-block, following-header, and following-granule evidence. Independent concealment comparison and audition remain external evidence |
| Verification gate for project VBRI output | Focused, orchestration, and hosted FFmpeg gates passed 2026-07-31 | A second deterministic MP3 fixture replaces the reserved first frame with VBRI metadata built from exact audio-relative frame offsets. The TOC uses one two-byte entry per frame. Declared stream bytes and frame count equal the complete memory and positional-file summaries, and the table is non-empty. The fake-tool runner requires this project probe and a nonsilent independent FFmpeg decode. Hosted Ubuntu run `30673501425` passed both the project VBRI decoder probe and independent FFmpeg decode. External VBRI input and audition remain open |
| Verification gate for fixed and dynamic matrix arithmetic consistency | Focused, public DSP, installed-package, and cross-target verification passed 2026-07-31 | Fixed `Matrix` provides checked subtraction alongside its existing addition and scaling operations. `DynamicMatrix` matches fixed matrix-vector multiplication through an allocating convenience call and a caller-buffer form with explicit workspace. Its addition, subtraction, scaling, transpose, and matrix multiplication now also expose caller-buffer forms. These forms permit safe source and destination overlap, reject workspace overlap, and preserve the destination after shape, non-finite input, arithmetic, or alias failure. An exhaustive allocation-failure sweep covers construction, cloning, arithmetic, matrix and vector products, LU, QR, tall and wide SVD, allocating solves, factors, inverses, pseudoinverses, and reconstruction; every partial owner is released. Focused matrix coverage passed 32/32 tests. The complete public DSP module passed 988/988 tests, and the staged installed-package consumer passed 63/63 while calling every caller-buffer matrix operation. The matrix suite compiles in ReleaseSafe mode for Linux AArch64 and Windows x86-64 GNU, and the broader plugin-core Windows cross-build passed |
| Verification gate for Linux AT-SPI object and capability publication | Native macOS, live Linux Debug and sanitizer, and hosted verification passed by 2026-08-01; assistive-technology confirmation remains | The Linux VSTGUI bridge discovers the accessibility bus without forcing service activation, connects through GIO, publishes the required `/org/a11y/atspi/accessible/root` object with Accessible, Application, and Component interfaces, registers stable child paths, and completes the registry `Embed` handshake including its application-ID write. Child objects publish Component plus capability-selected Action, Value, Text, and EditableText interfaces. The required cache object returns the application root and children in one typed bulk query, emits one typed addition for each object after successful embedding, and emits child-first removals before teardown. Failed startup emits no cache lifecycle signals. Queries cover hierarchy, names, descriptions, roles, state bitsets including dynamic truncation, locale, stable IDs, help text, interface names, bounds, hit testing, focus, actions, numeric ranges, Unicode-segmented single-line text, ordinary and transformed glyph geometry, caret positions, and one normalized selection. Value writes, actions, caret movement, selection changes, whole-text replacement, UTF-8 character-position insertion, deletion, and clipboard-backed copy, cut, and paste use the toolkit-neutral action path on the editor thread. Clipboard access is an owned provider contract with an X11 platform default. Pasted text is limited to 1 MiB and must be complete UTF-8 without embedded NUL bytes. Out-of-range actions, invalid character positions and ranges, truncated UTF-8 input, clipboard read or write failures, rejected providers, and non-finite numeric writes preserve state and avoid unintended plugin callbacks. Node observers publish property, state, focus, bounds, text-caret, text-selection, and Unicode-delta text-change events and are detached before D-Bus object teardown. The existing editor timer performs bounded main-context and XCB selection dispatch. Missing session, accessibility-bus, registry, or X display services fail independently without changing the toolkit-neutral nodes. An isolated D-Bus fixture verifies missing accessibility-bus and registry recovery, address discovery, registration, application ID, traversal, interface discovery, bounds, action invocation, numeric value access, Unicode Text queries, segmentation, deltas, rotated geometry, head and tail truncation, truncation state transitions, caret movement, forward and backward selection, provider rejection, complete UTF-8 text edits, clipboard provider success and failure, hostile-input containment, bulk cache contents, cache lifecycle signals, native events, observer detachment, and teardown. The complete transport target passed in Debug and sanitizer builds in an AArch64 Ubuntu container, the native macOS interaction and accessibility target passed, and the separate Xvfb gate proves default X11 clipboard exchange. Built-in visual caret and selection synchronization, pure-Wayland clipboard ownership, Orca or Accerciser confirmation, and visual confirmation remain open |
| Verification gate for Windows host validation | Complete local, Linux, and Windows host confirmation passed 2026-07-31 | Both downstream Windows hosts originally terminated while scanning the first x86-64 GNU ARA bundle. The official validator loaded the module and enumerated all three classes before terminating at `Scan Editor Classes`; pluginval terminated at its first scan of the same bundle. The last complete successful run predated the product's inline spectral transform integration. That integration retained 4,096 frames across four published generations, processing scratch, and source storage. A controlled regression that constructs the effect on a 1 MiB host stack reproduces the old capacity as an abort. The product demonstration now bounds the same capability to 1,024 frames, reducing `SpectralSource` to 123,912 bytes, with a focused size regression capped at 160 KiB. The first CI run proved that the capacity reduction alone was insufficient on Linux because the generic construction path returned the large processor value through three nested initializers. Plugin instances, processor runtimes, and the format adapter now expose a direct in-place construction chain, and effect construction selects it before any return-by-value compatibility path. The bounded-stack product regression passes locally and in the complete Ubuntu matrix. The focused product gate, full ARA gate, installed-package consumers, complete local gate, native macOS and Windows matrices, official Windows validator, and Windows pluginval all pass. Both Windows hosts complete the ARA bundle scan that previously terminated |
| Verification gate for VSTGUI timing stability | Repeated native timing and correctness verification passed 2026-07-31 | Five consecutive isolated runs and one complete rebuilt native gate passed with the existing budgets unchanged. Across the isolated runs, ordinary warm rendering measured 93.8–102.8 microseconds, signal views measured 278.1–289.3 microseconds, and Sample Player editor construction and destruction measured 51.7–53.7 milliseconds. The rebuilt complete run measured 99.3 microseconds, 287.6 microseconds, and 53.9 milliseconds respectively. The native interaction, accessibility, and snapshot comparisons also passed. The earlier 15–98 millisecond lifecycle spread and intermittent graph overruns are not reproducible in the current tree; no threshold or harness change is justified |
| Verification gate for DSP fixture timing stability | Complete local and live Linux gates passed 2026-07-31 | A single 8,192-frame timing sample normally measured about 2.5 ns/sample, but one preempted f64 sample crossed the unchanged 100 ns/sample ceiling and returned before reporting its measurement. Fixed and randomized partitions now run five measured trials and retain the fastest complete trial, which rejects persistent regressions without treating one scheduler interruption as processor cost. Both measurements print before threshold enforcement. Exact f32, f64, and block-partition parity checks remain unchanged and still run before performance acceptance. The corrected live Linux run measured about 1.8 ns/sample for both f32 and f64 fixed and randomized partitions against the unchanged 100 ns/sample ceiling |
| Verification gate for external Vorbis encoder input | Complete local and live Linux gates passed 2026-07-31; hosted damaged-packet rejection and recovery passed 2026-08-01 | The bounded probe decodes project-generated mono and independently encoded mono, stereo, and 5.1 streams, including compatible project and external chains. It validates complete per-stream headers, EOS, continuous PCM positions, finite nonsilent output, matching caller-owned memory and file seek indexes, and exact interleaved f32 suffixes after positional seeks. The first live seek run exposed unpositioned overlap-prime output before a page granule established the PCM range. A second run showed that a target immediately before the first indexed page end had the right frame count but incorrect overlap content because the first seek point anchored near the end of a packed page. Sequential and positional comparison now discard only unpositioned prime output, and the first seek point for each logical stream retains its first audio packet while later points keep their shorter overlap-prime anchors. Local project, chained-project, checksum-damage, later-logical-stream checksum-damage, truncation, fake-tool, public DSP, Linux cross-build, and Windows cross-build gates pass. The live Linux matrix passes external 8 kHz 512/512, 16 kHz 512/1024, and 44.1 kHz low-quality 512/4096 block geometries, rejects a chained geometry change, and retains mono, stereo, 5.1, compatible chains, short-stream, and required-midpoint seek cases. A bounded transport recovery probe inserts hostile bytes at a complete-packet page boundary. Memory and positional-file readers reject a five-byte search bound without advancing, accept the exact six-byte bound, and reproduce every packet and transport field from the clean stream. Hosted Ubuntu run `30689183600` passed strict invalid-audio rejection and explicit known-block concealment while preserving finite nonsilent later audio. Separate policies infer missing geometry from preceding-block, following-header, and following-granule evidence. Independent concealment comparison and audition remain open |
| Verification gate for independent MP3 encoder input | Complete local, macOS, and live Linux gates passed 2026-07-31 | The bounded decoder probe compares memory and positional-file summaries, including audio extents, frame and sample counts, sample rate, channels, Xing fields, and VBRI presence. It then decodes every frame through both paths, validates final gapless counts, rejects non-finite or silent output, and requires exact decoded f32 evidence across memory and file traversal. The project-generated 44.1 kHz stereo fixture passes the probe and independent FFmpeg and AudioToolbox decoding. Live Linux uses FFmpeg with libmp3lame to generate MPEG-1 44.1 kHz stereo VBR, MPEG-2 22.05 kHz mono CBR, and MPEG-2.5 8 kHz mono CBR fixtures; all three pass complete project decoding with exact rate and channel assertions. Independent FFmpeg f32 references match exact sample-value counts for all three versions. MPEG-1 compares 30,870 values with `4.32e-7` peak error and `5.56e-7` normalized RMS error. MPEG-2 compares 7,718 values with `6.56e-7` peak error and `9.27e-7` normalized RMS error. MPEG-2.5 compares 2,800 values with `1.151e-6` peak error and `2.608e-6` normalized RMS error. The first reference run exposed one untrimmed Xing metadata frame. The aligned run then exposed the standard 529-sample decoder-latency redistribution between delay and padding. Readers and writers now apply both conventions symmetrically. A separate 2.2-second 44.1 kHz stereo CBR fixture carries an exact ID3v2.3 prefix and ID3v1 tail, includes both padded and unpadded MPEG frames, produces matching multi-point memory and file seek indexes, and passes a midpoint positional seek with matching frame, sample, and byte state. Another independently encoded fixture carries an exact ID3v2.4 prefix and passes complete memory and file decoding. The same gate rejects a truncated external stream and a concatenated format change. A dedicated external trailing-junk fixture proves that memory and positional-file readers reject the malformed tail without advancing frame or sample state or writing caller frame storage. The fake-tool runner proves that every positive, negative, and reference probe is required, and the complete local aggregate passes. External VBRI coverage and audition remain open |

Generic layouts use nominal-position convex hulls with measured-position triplet and quadrilateral regions, direct-downmix virtual layer speakers, and normalized top and bottom virtual-speaker downmixes. Stereo uses the dedicated five-to-two-channel path with rear-dependent attenuation. LFE outputs retain their layout indexes but receive no panner gain. Gain calculation and mixing support f32 and f64, reject malformed layouts and non-finite requests, normalize generic-layout power, and preflight all buffer shapes and aliases before mutation. `AdmDirectSpeakerPositionRouter` now exposes polar-only and unified polar/Cartesian fallback mixers while preserving direct and discard routes. Tests cover exact physical speakers, adapted reproduction positions, screen-speaker nominal correction, virtual coverage, true quadrilateral facets, stereo front and rear behavior, LFE exclusion, duplicate nominal and measured positions, invalid state, non-finite directions, aliases, and end-to-end DirectSpeakers fallback. The file builder also bounds every second-pass destination write so a file that gains seek points between its sizing and construction scans reports `Mp3SeekIndexChanged` instead of indexing beyond caller storage. The MP3-focused set passed 11/11. The single-process public DSP module passed 659/659, and installed-package consumers passed 51/51 with one Zig build job. No GUI or host process was launched |
| Current sequential gate after transactional Ogg page and packet parsing | Passed focused and installed-package verification 2026-07-27; later complete gate passed 2026-08-02 | `OggPageIterator.next` now parses against copied state and commits its cursor, stream identity, sequence, continuation, EOS, and chained-stream index only after the whole page validates. A malformed page after EOS can no longer partially advance the logical-stream state. `OggPacketIterator.next` likewise commits page and segment cursors only after a complete packet is available. A packet buffer that fills after one or more 255-byte segments can be replaced and retried without losing the already-read prefix. Memory page and packet readers reject corrupted public cursors explicitly, and packet-count rollover returns an error without advancement. The file-backed page reader proves the remaining `u64` extent contains a complete header before positional reads or offset additions, so a short tail near the maximum file offset returns `TruncatedOggPage` instead of trapping on arithmetic overflow. The file packet reader validates paired storage bindings, retained page and reload cursors, packet capacity, and counters before optional dereferences or slice indexing. It now retains an allocation-free positional checkpoint at each packet boundary. A late capacity failure can clear the old storage bindings, reconstruct the page parser state, reload a multi-page or packed chained-BOS packet, and retry with larger storage while preserving global and logical packet indexes plus seek-skip accounting. The single-process public DSP module passed 657/657, including multi-page and packed chained-page late capacity retries, hostile memory and file packet state, packet-count rollover, maximum file offset, past-end cursor, chained-tail rollback, and ordinary file-backed streaming coverage. Installed-package consumers passed 51/51 with one Zig build job. No GUI or host process was launched |

Equal identification block sizes select the short-mode representation. `VorbisPcmBlockLookahead` retains one decision to provide the pending block's right-window flag, while `VorbisPcmFramePlanner` selects the lowest retained mapping mode, carries the prior block choice, and advances signed centers by the Vorbis quarter-current plus quarter-next rule. `extractVorbisPcmBlock` validates and zero-pads signed source windows at stream boundaries. `VorbisPcmBlockTransform` applies precomputed transition windows and normalized forward MDCT to every channel through caller scratch, then commits all outputs together. Tests cover stationary, silent, attacked, and non-finite PCM; both precisions; invalid thresholds and block shapes; equal block sizes; unavailable and malformed modes; short, large-to-short, short-to-large, and large-to-large sequences; lookahead priming, finishing, reset, and failure preservation; signed leading and trailing extraction; position and packet-count containment; source, output, and scratch aliases; exact input/output overlap; short scratch; channel-transactional failure; and direct short and long transform parity. Installed-package coverage analyzes, schedules, extracts, plans, and transforms through public types. Cross-block detector tuning, psychoacoustic and rate-distortion policy, bitrate management, independent Xiph packet parity, and third-party file interoperability remain open |
| Verification gate after setup-driven Vorbis Floor 1 fitting | Passed 2026-07-27 | 323/323 build steps and 5,859/5,860 tests passed with one expected CoreAudio service-availability skip. Installed-package consumers passed 48/48, the public VST3 module passed 755/755, the public DSP surface passed 1,170/1,170, and the complete Ogg/Vorbis module passed 49/49. `fitVorbisFloorOne` converts a finite f32 or f64 target spectrum into raw Floor 1 values through retained control-point geometry. It searches active masterbook paths and subclass entries, evaluates the decoder's exact integer prediction and unwrap rules, resolves ties to the lowest legal entry, and preflights the result through the packet writer before committing caller output. Silent input produces the unused-floor encoding without output storage. `normalizeVorbisResidue` transactionally divides signed spectra by positive synthesized floor curves, supports exact in-place use, and composes with `applyVorbisFloor` for reconstruction. Tests cover exact raw-value packet round trips, synthesis parity, both float precisions, signed residue normalization and reconstruction, silent input, unused destination suffixes, insufficient capacity, invalid shapes and values, unencodable books, wrong floor types, setup and target aliases, partial normalization aliases, non-positive curves, and exact in-place normalization. Installed-package coverage fits, synthesizes, and normalizes through public types. Automatic PCM block analysis, cross-block floor policy, psychoacoustic and rate-distortion decisions, bitrate management, independent Xiph packet parity, and third-party file interoperability remain open |

| Verification gate after setup-driven Vorbis residue quantization | Passed 2026-07-27 | 323/323 build steps and 5,857/5,858 tests passed with one expected CoreAudio service-availability skip. Installed-package consumers passed 48/48, the public VST3 module passed 755/755, the public DSP surface passed 1,168/1,168, and the complete Ogg/Vorbis module passed 47/47. `requiredVorbisResidueQuantizationScratch` reports exact caller-owned partition, vector, and classification storage. `quantizeVorbisResidue` searches only classification groups representable by active classbook entries, simulates every active cascade pass, greedily quantizes the current residual through retained lookup type 1 or type 2 vector books, and emits classifications and entries in the packet writer's exact eight-pass order. | Verification gate after transactional paged ARA source caching | Passed 2026-07-27 | 308/308 build steps and 5,630/5,633 tests passed with three expected macOS audio and MIDI service-availability skips. Memory and positional-file paths agree on summaries and decoded f32 output. Independent FFmpeg PCM comparisons have exact sample counts. Shine output without an optional Info frame produced 32,256 interleaved samples with `4.02e-7` peak error and `2.99e-7` normalized RMS error. Protected LAME output produced 30,870 samples with every frame CRC validated, `3.73e-7` peak error, and `3.69e-7` normalized RMS error. A separate 320 kb/s LAME free-format stream produced 30,870 samples with `2.1026e-5` peak error and `8.0562e-5` normalized RMS error against LAME's decoder. FFmpeg rejects that free-format input, so it is not used as its PCM oracle. The parser treats zero Lavc delay and padding fields as unspecified and reads Xing or Info metadata at the fixed post-side-information location for protected and unprotected frames. A recovery probe inserts five junk bytes between two frames of an external FFmpeg stream, proves a four-byte search bound preserves state, accepts the exact five-byte bound, and reproduces the uninterrupted PCM evidence. Record completed host observations in [host-matrix.md](host-matrix.md). The detailed walkthroughs and limitations are in [real-host-coverage.md](real-host-coverage.md), [release-checklist.md](release-checklist.md), and [gui-sample-player-plan.md](gui-sample-player-plan.md).

## Cleanup And API Debt

| Item | State | Scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Remove accumulated build artifacts | Completed 2026-08-04 | After explicit approval, the reviewed cleaners removed the 95,674,476 KiB autonomous Zig cache and all 33 completed installed-package staging trees. No source, Git history, package output, active staging tree, or external cache was removed. Available space increased from 19 GiB to 190 GiB. The complete cold verification chain rebuilt a 1,026,036 KiB cache and leaves 182 GiB available with the volume at 80 percent. The real installed-package suite passes twice at 85/85 tests across 12/12 steps, and post-run inspection finds no completed staging tree. The autonomous cache is ignored by Git. The 64 GiB preflight prevents an oversized run from starting, and the postflight reports growth caused by the completed or failed run without masking a compiler failure. |
| Review unchecked declaration constructors before compatibility freeze | Complete 2026-08-03 | `FloatParam.init`, `LogFloatParam.init`, `IntParam.init`, and `PluginSpec.init` accept only compile-time arguments and report invalid fixed declarations as compiler errors. Runtime input uses the corresponding checked constructor and propagates validation errors. The parameter and plugin-interface guides document both paths. Installed-package consumers pass 82/82 across 12/12 steps, supported plugin-core cross-target builds pass, and the complete ReleaseSafe graph passes 400/400 steps and 7,353/7,359 tests with six environment-dependent skips. No host, device, audible, visual, or manual confirmation is required |
| Harden nullable raw callback arguments | Completed by the current raw callback containment gate above | Public query declarations, generated and handwritten implementations, shared lookup, custom dispatch, host-provided structures, identifiers, events, buffers, scalar outputs, and GUI telemetry buffers use null-capable boundary types with direct rejection regressions. The public VST3 module, raw API ABI, selected interface ABI matrix, plugin-core cross-target build, installed-package suite, policy scan, and policy self-test pass. Continue enforcing this through the complete test graph and CI hygiene job |
| Purge retired third-party comparison material from Git history | Completed for every writable ref 2026-07-28 | Rewritten `main`, `feature/plugin-gui`, and `zig-vst3-0.2.1` are published and the active local repository is migrated. The remaining read-only GitHub pull refs are tracked under Repository History Sanitization and do not block development |
| Consolidate internal segmented-message helpers | Completed 2026-07-28 | SysEx7, SysEx8, endpoint text, Flex text, and Mixed Data Sets share only overflow-safe packet boundaries, packet-form selection, base reassembly phases, transactional storage updates, and completion transitions. Protocol-specific identity, payload, packet-limit, text-validation, and error contracts remain separate |
| Review the flat `process.zig` export surface | Completed 2026-07-28 | `process.midi` supplies a structured protocol namespace while the original flat modules and type aliases remain available. Public-core and installed-package coverage verify both paths |
| Improve Windows MSVC C-kernel prerequisite detection | Completed 2026-07-28 | Non-Windows MSVC targets without `--libc` fail during configuration with instructions for generating an MSVC libc description and with the Windows GNU alternative. The focused runner covers missing, configured, and GNU cases, and the complete gate includes it |
| Review the expanded DSP API before compatibility freeze | Completed 2026-07-28 | Root configuration aliases and invalid-configuration errors use domain-qualified names. Generic fixed-storage types require explicit product-selected capacities. Public configuration and caller-storage limits have flat package constants; internal staging counts do not. Polynomial capacity and root defaults are introspectable, iteration exhaustion is reported without discarding ordered candidates, and invalid or stalled solves remain errors |
| Review the ADM Objects timeline API before compatibility freeze | Pending after external renderer comparison | Confirm whether products need a document-to-channel collector, an object-origin sample offset, a cursor-optimized strictly sequential mixer in addition to arbitrary-range mixing, or a conventional default block capacity. Keep the current compile-time capacity, exact-time, endpoint-precomputation, and allocation-free audio-thread contracts unless real integration evidence requires a change |
| Review the ADM diffuse processor API and performance before compatibility freeze | Representative f32 and f64 profiling completed 2026-07-29; external comparison remains | The permanent benchmark covers a fully active 12-channel height layout at 16, 64, and 512 frames. After replacing software-f128 convolution accumulation with f64, three runs measure 318.4–350.3 ns per f32 output sample and 341.3–366.6 ns per f64 output sample on this machine, roughly 28 times faster at 16 frames. Dense f64 output remains within `2e-14` of an f128 reference. The small-block penalty is under eight percent, so a bounded FFT path is not justified by current measurements. Confirm the 255-sample direct-delay convention against independent renderers and decide whether products need additive final-output mixing before freezing the API |
| Review the ADM common-layout routing API before compatibility freeze | Pending external renderer comparison | Confirm whether products should supply a stable typed output-layout identity instead of the current bounded name, and whether a document-to-channel collector should derive each common input pack automatically. Review the new multi-output `DirectSpeakerRoute` case before freezing exhaustive-switch compatibility. Keep rule ordering, symmetric expansion, complete-destination matching, and allocation-free mixing intact |
| Review the ADM Matrix coefficient API before compatibility freeze | Automated contract defined; external comparison and product sizing remain | The opt-in variable path uses caller-named typed lanes, exact absolute sample positions, held or linear interpolation, linear gain, phase degrees, rounded delay endpoints with fractional motion, and a caller-designed antisymmetric quadrature FIR with reported group-delay latency. The renderer-standard constant-gain and integer-delay path remains separate and unchanged. Confirm representative maximum delay, variable, point, and FIR capacities, determine whether products need shared history for many simultaneous outputs, and compare the phase convention with independent tools before freezing the API |
| Review measured-distance HRTF neighbor selection before compatibility freeze | Pending multi-distance dataset comparison and profiling | Angular and three-dimensional queries currently share a fixed three-neighbor result and reconstruction path while retaining separate bounded selection loops. Keep them separate until a licensed multi-distance dataset confirms distance weighting and profiling establishes whether consolidation improves maintenance without obscuring the single-radius compatibility rule. Review the `HrtfMeasurementPosition` name, the one-metre constructor default, and product capacity choices before freezing the API |
| Finish VST3 runtime example migration | Completed 2026-07-26 | All twelve restart categories and the common resource-path, decoded-audio import, scalar, graph, text, and editor-visibility payload families pass through the high-level adapter. Fixed Rate, IR Loader, Resource Swap, Sample Player, Channel Strip, Parametric EQ, Resonant Filter, and Model Shell cover the specialized product paths. Model Shell uses stable high-level ownership for its self-referential recovery worker while preserving bounded component state, resource commands, latency approval, scalar and text telemetry, and both processing precisions. Gain, Bypass, Mode Gain, Voice Mix, Note Gate, Event Monitor, Sine Synth, and Editor Smoke remain deliberately low-level conformance probes |
| Consolidate VST3 high-level configuration | Completed 2026-07-26 | `Vst3Effect` derives main and auxiliary topology, event buses, host-transport requirements, parameter metadata, and the runtime adapter from one plugin declaration. `Vst3Controller` does the same for ordinary reflected controllers. Configured-parameter variants own parameter sets built from the same descriptor value as the runtime. Twelve high-level examples use the constructor; the C-kernel probe remains a direct `SimpleEffect` conformance integration |
| Consolidate file-backed PCM I/O internals | Completed 2026-07-27 | Direct and dithered PCM conversion, signed 16-, 24-, and 32-bit byte-order handling, IEEE f32 encoding and decoding, width queries, fixed 4 KiB staging, positional short-write retry, truncation, fault injection, dither channel-phase handling, alignment-padding output, and checked exact or aligned checkpoint restoration are shared across WAV, AIFF, RF64, Wave64, and the bounded reader. Cross-container failure tests prove RIFF and AIFF word padding, Wave64 eight-byte alignment, partial-write rollback, dither rollback, header recovery, short-write retry, retry after restoration failure, and rejection of inconsistent committed boundaries before truncation. The writers correctly retain format-shaped size commits and header repair |
| Consolidate file-backed audio helpers | Exact reads, bounded whole-file reads, writer checkpoints, and RIFF-family traversal completed 2026-07-29; format-specific streaming remains | PCM, FLAC, Ogg, and MP3 readers share exact positional reads and preserve format-specific truncation errors through thin adapters. Bounded whole-file FLAC entry points also share capacity and exact-read handling. WAV, RF64, and BW64 audio and metadata paths share one checked RIFF chunk iterator; Wave64 audio and metadata paths share a separate eight-byte-aligned iterator. PCM, FLAC, and Ogg writers share validated committed-boundary restoration while retaining format-specific commit semantics. AIFF, streaming FLAC, Ogg, and MP3 traversal remains separate because their framing and retained-state contracts differ |
| Consolidate ALSA runtime loading and discovery | Candidate cleanup after native Linux validation | The optional PCM, MIDI 1, and UMP shims intentionally keep independent libasound handles and lifecycle state, but duplicate bounded identifier copying, directional discovery, default selection, and symbol-loading mechanics. Share only the common control-thread helpers without coupling the optional modules, violating Zig package module ownership, or adding libasound to ordinary plugin binaries |
| Consolidate Linux window backend scaffolding | Candidate cleanup after native Linux validation | X11 and Wayland intentionally retain independent native lifecycles, but duplicate bounded UTF-8 title storage, erased backend forwarding, injected lifecycle tests, runtime symbol loading, and cross-target link-smoke construction. Share only the format-neutral pieces after physical behavior is established |
| Consolidate Wayland client loading after live clipboard validation | Candidate cleanup after native Linux validation | The standalone window shim and accessibility clipboard transport intentionally own separate connections and protocol lifecycles, but duplicate safe dynamic-symbol loading and core interface declarations. After live compositor coverage establishes both paths, extract only the loader mechanics without sharing mutable connection, proxy, event, or teardown state |
| Stabilize the standalone backend API | Format-neutral fault and teardown contracts covered 2026-07-28; native confirmation remains | CoreAudio, CoreMIDI, WASAPI, WinMM, ALSA PCM, PipeWire, ALSA MIDI 1, and ALSA UMP fix unified versus directional identifiers, stable-address callback lifetimes, native or polled topology generations, timestamp and format conversion, planar buffer ownership, stop ordering, retained error statistics, and failure sources. The format-neutral recovery coordinator provides transactional fallback, retry, preferred-device restoration, and bounded multi-source failure monitoring. MIDI 1 and UMP retained block buffers expose checked public counts and recoverable views, and schedulers reject malformed destinations before consuming queued packets. Continuous-phase sinc resampling, a bounded proportional-integral fill controller, the retained multichannel SPSC capture FIFO, the synchronized capture-rate processing bridge, and the split processor callback adapter supply the complete format-neutral correction path. Application fixtures cover callback drain during stop, stale-callback silence after stop or failed start, processor rollback, restart, and retry. WASAPI, ALSA, PipeWire, and CoreAudio expose the split path through independently timed native callbacks. Validate native runtime behavior, including PipeWire and Windows MIDI Services, and decide whether Linux needs ALSA sequencer scheduling |
| Stabilize the LV2 Worker hook API | Internal contract stabilized 2026-07-28; external host confirmation remains | Hook names and size bounds are documented. The adapter owns the schedule sink for the instance lifetime, limits scheduling to `run`, and limits each response sink to its worker callback. Dynamic-library fixtures cover host copies across callback lifetimes, a separate worker thread overlapping a later audio block, delayed response delivery after `run`, end-of-cycle ordering, drained teardown, missing features, and immediate freewheeling delivery. Confirm the same ownership and scheduling behavior in external hosts before the compatibility promise |
| Stabilize the LV2 host-feature and logging API | Source, parser, installed-package, focused dynamic-host, and independent C ABI evidence completed 2026-08-02; external-host confirmation remains | Core and UI construction reject a null feature-array pointer. Core, state, and UI paths reject misaligned, unterminated, or malformed bounded arrays plus duplicate required or configuration-bearing host features before creating an instance or invoking a state callback. Known feature and interface URIs use bounded exact comparison rather than unbounded host-string slicing. Ambiguous duplicate optional UI callback features are disabled. The instance-owned logging sink exposes non-realtime error, warning, and note writes separately from realtime-safe trace writes, rejects interior NUL bytes, and always passes messages through a fixed string format. Dynamic and installed-package tests use a C variadic callback to verify the exact format and expanded payload through the public ABI. Keep the sink naming, nullable return contract, 256-entry scan bound, feature-uniqueness policy, and compile-time `bindLv2Log` opt-in provisional until at least two external hosts confirm log presentation and ordinary feature discovery |
| Stabilize the LV2 metadata API | Current schema and warning-fatal direct-distribution lint completed 2026-08-06; external-host confirmation remains | GitHub Actions run 31071465341 validated the current Mono Gain bundle and frozen dynamic-topology bundle with LV2 1.18.10 and `lv2lint` 0.16.2. The schema validator found zero errors across 1,964 restrictions for Mono Gain and 1,972 for dynamic topology, and both warning-fatal direct-distribution lint invocations passed. This covers the current static float, peak, and typed event port notifications plus main, sidechain, auxiliary, common discrete, and bounded ACN port-group triples. Project, license, maintainer, description, live-use, preset, Programs, UI feature, UI extension, and notification declarations are generated from the public metadata API. Notification symbols must identify a compatible generated port, event notifications on one output must have distinct Atom type URIs, and source validation rejects invalid URI schemes and Turtle control characters. Confirm discovery and presentation in external hosts, then freeze audio-port symbol conventions, preset declaration naming, notification policy, and version fields |
| Stabilize the LV2 UI adapter API | Toolkit-neutral lifecycle and bounded bidirectional typed transport covered internally 2026-08-03; native host confirmation remains | The dynamic toolkit-neutral fixture covers two simultaneous instances, close and reopen, 64 lifecycle cycles, idle, host resize, show, hide, automation, touch, static and dynamic peaks, typed Atom event-output delivery, typed event-input writes, invalid construction, malformed events, and hostile handles. Validate VSTGUI parent and widget ownership plus bidirectional typed message discovery and delivery in external hosts, then decide whether repaint and context-menu features need host extensions |
| Stabilize the ARA controller, extension, cache, and renderer API | Internal hostile-host pass completed 2026-07-28; external host review remains | Factory hook reentrancy, controller-slot reservation and rollback, extension failure containment, observer mutation during publication, archive compatibility, corrupt-input rollback, and restore retry are covered internally. External integration must still review fixed-capacity defaults, singleton and controller ownership, provider and extension lifetimes, multi-reader and cache sizing, paging policy, void-callback error reporting, transformation declarations, render scratch limits, interpolation quality, and host thread guarantees |
| Reconcile public API guides before each tag | Ongoing | Keep README, framework guides, stability notes, changelog, and release checklist aligned |

## Feature Backlog

| Area | Remaining work |
| --- | --- |
| Vorbis encoder orchestration | Complete bounded PCM-to-packet construction is implemented. `VorbisPcmPacketSequence` stages classification, lookahead, frame, budget, packet index, reservoir, and completed-center granules. It can optionally adapt each nonterminal packet target from activity, internal or cross-block transient strength, and crest factor while retaining explicit product thresholds and reservoir bounds. `VorbisQualityRateController` feeds committed packet size, residue-budget evidence, and measured audible excess back into bounded psychoacoustic quality with caller-selected limits, adjustment, and headroom. `VorbisPcmFrameAnalyzer` extracts planned boundaries, applies the multichannel windowed MDCT, and publishes Bark-domain floor and masking analysis into caller storage. `encodeVorbisPcmPacket` composes that analysis with Floor 1 fitting, residue and masking-threshold normalization, forward coupling, adaptive submap quantization, exact packet sizing, and reservoir preflight without advancing the live sequence. Memory and rollback-safe file append publish the sequence only after the packet destination accepts it. Configuration, capacity, setup, arithmetic, quantization, packet, stale-plan, impossible-budget, terminal-granule, and quality-feedback failures are covered transactionally. FFmpeg and Xiph independently decode every q0, q5, and q10 project fixture and pass direct source-relative objective-quality bounds. Strict damaged-packet rejection, opt-in silence and signal-derived concealment, and three geometry-inference policies are implemented separately from the encoder. Independent Xiph packet parity, concealment comparison, and audition remain separate closing evidence |
| Bus topology | Plugin declarations, bounded topology and snapshot types, process contexts, VST3, static LV2 and AUv2 buses, Audio Unit rendering, live and offline standalone runtimes, device routing, and native PCM backend storage accept compile-time capacities from zero through 254 auxiliary buses per direction. The source-compatible default remains eight. The 39 non-empty layouts cover mono, five stereo roles, cinematic and music surround variants through 7.1, 5.x and 7.x immersive layouts through 7.1.4, and first- through seventh-order ACN ambisonics. VST3 metadata, arrangement negotiation, activation, flush validation, realtime processing, backward-compatible versioned state, controller-readable state, immutable snapshot publication, and coalesced `kIoChanged` restarts share one generation. LV2 and AUv2 use static format declarations rather than live insertion and removal. Real-host confirmation of high-count configurations remains |
| Plugin formats | The format-neutral processor and editor lifecycles, offline and standalone consumers, VST3 processor adapter, LV2 core adapter, and toolkit-neutral LV2 UI bridge are implemented. Mono Gain has a generated LV2 bundle with declaration-derived ports, a designated toggled freewheeling control, checked presets, and validated project and distribution metadata. Declared program lists now drive bounded Programs core and UI interfaces plus generated metadata. Plugins that inspect host-defined Atom types may explicitly require and retain the standard URI reverse-mapping feature. Dynamic fixtures cover block-boundary realtime and offline mode propagation, missing and malformed controls, bounded component state, typed host logging, immediate offline and delayed threaded Worker delivery, response lifetime and end-of-cycle ordering, program enumeration and selection, simultaneous UI instances, 64-cycle close and reopen stress, parameter, touch, idle, show, hide, and resize callbacks. Generated Turtle advertises optional host logging and supports separate UI resources and binaries and passes independent schema and warning-fatal lint validation. The native desktop bundle links the VSTGUI parameter backend, publishes the correct UI class and binary, and validates both exported descriptor symbols. Independent C ABI checks and Linux and Windows cross-builds include the toolkit-neutral UI declarations and fixture. The AUv2 path covers negotiated lifecycle, f32 and f64 deinterleaved processing, static main and auxiliary input and output buses, transport, latency, tail, deterministic failure silence, reflected parameter metadata, immediate, sample-offset, and ramp automation, Core Foundation class-info state, input pull, property listeners, render notifications, timestamp-keyed multi-output caching, owning component factories, and registered Mono Gain and Auxiliary Output Splitter `.component` bundles. SDK ABI, cross-target component builds, plist and symbol checks, and two dynamic-library host fixtures pass. Add real-host LV2 reverse-mapping, Worker, and VSTGUI UI validation, then consider AUv3 or AAX |
| Standalone | The allocation-free block contract, bounded offline renderer, checked live callback runtime, flat-device-to-bus routing, timestamped MIDI 1 and complete-UMP device contracts and scheduling, rollback-safe application lifecycle, directional device catalog, version-2 selection persistence with version-1 restore, transactional fallback and restart coordination, preferred-device restoration, bounded multi-source failure monitoring, continuous-phase sinc rate correction, a bounded clock-drift fill controller, a bounded multichannel SPSC capture FIFO, a synchronized capture-rate processing bridge, a split capture and render processor adapter, bounded top-level window and event-loop contracts, macOS CoreAudio with dual-AUHAL split devices, CoreMIDI, an AppKit top-level window, Windows shared-mode WASAPI with native notifications and split capture/render callbacks, Windows timestamp-scheduled WinMM MIDI 1 and native complete UMP through the optional MIDI Services App SDK, a Win32 top-level window, Linux ALSA PCM with native split capture and playback workers, Linux PipeWire with bounded graph discovery and combined or split planar streams, Linux timestamp-scheduled ALSA RawMIDI and UMP RawMIDI with topology polling, runtime-loaded X11 and Wayland top-level windows, a shared VST3 Wayland host/frame bridge, and the Steinberg run-loop driver are implemented. Physically validate PipeWire, native clock correction, MIDI scheduler timing, cancellation, and Windows UMP service and endpoint behavior |
| MIDI | macOS CoreMIDI endpoint discovery, topology generations, MIDI 1 channel-voice input, checked parser rejection, and timestamped output are connected to the standalone contracts. Linux ALSA supplies native timestamped MIDI 1 and complete UMP transports. Windows supplies native complete UMP transport through the optional MIDI Services App SDK. The format-neutral UMP boundary preserves and schedules every packet width. Property Exchange advertises common-rules version 1.2 and includes bounded ResourceList metadata, DeviceInfo, ChannelList, JSONSchema, paginated ProgramList, ModeList, CurrentMode, ChannelMode, BasicChannelRx, BasicChannelTx, LocalOn, ExternalSync, StateList, State reply metadata, AllCtrlList, ChCtrlList, and CtrlMapList models. Independent-device interoperability remains open. Physically validate native Windows service startup, endpoint direction, packet widths, timestamps, scheduling, hot-plug recovery, teardown, and delivery |
